It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that</p>"},{"timestamp":"11:50:31 AM","text":"<p>While this occurred when I was a child, I knew it was wrong, and I believe it was very morally wrong. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part. I was nice to everyone else as a child, and very well-behaved otherwise! I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part. I was nice to everyone else as a child, and very well-behaved otherwise! More than that, I think I was overall pretty kind and focused on trying to do what is right. This was, obviously, though, very wrong. I certainly am not at risk of doing anything like this again, but it's weird to know that I did do this. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. Maybe getting wax out I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part as a child. I was nice to everyone else, and very well-behaved otherwise! More than that, I think I was overall pretty kind and focused on trying to do what is right. This was, obviously, though, very wrong. I certainly am not at risk of doing anything like this again, but it's weird to know that I did do this. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out of carpet is quite difficult. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. He was very young so I don't think</p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part as a child. I was nice to everyone else, and very well-behaved otherwise! More than that, I think I was overall pretty kind and focused on trying to do what is right. This was, obviously, though, very wrong. I certainly am not at risk of doing anything like this again, but it's weird to know that I did do this. I think this makes me realize that people really can change as moral agents. </p>"},{"timestamp":"11:57:32 AM","text":"<p>While this occurred when I was a child, I knew it was wrong, and I believe it was very morally wrong. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out of carpet is quite difficult. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. He was very young so I don't think he fully understood, but he understood enough for this to be distressing.</p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this but I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out of carpet is quite difficult. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. He was very young so I don't think he fully understood, but he understood enough for this to be distressing. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this; I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part as a child. I was nice to everyone else, and very well-behaved otherwise! It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out of carpet is quite difficult. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. He was very young so I don't think he fully understood, but he understood enough for this to be distressing. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this; I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part as a child. I was nice to everyone else, and very well-behaved otherwise! More than that, I think I was overall pretty kind and focused on trying to do what is right. This was, obviously, though, very wrong. I certainly am not at risk of doing anything like this again, but it's weird to know that I did do this. I think this makes me realize that people really can change as moral agents. My great-grandfather passed away when I was young, and it was the first death I really experienced. I have a younger brother who is five years younger than me, and so he was quite young. After his death, we were visiting my great-grandmother, who I found sort of scary and intimidating. She was odd and loud and not very warm. I saw some melted red candle wax on her floor. It had been there for a while, and I don't know why it was there or why no one ever tried to clean it up. I do imagine that getting wax out of carpet is quite difficult. I told my brother that this was blood from our great-grandfather and that our great-grandmother had killed him. My brother was, of course, quite upset about this. He was very young so I don't think he fully understood, but he understood enough for this to be distressing. </p><p><br></p><p>It's impossible for me to rationalize this; I certainly knew this wasn't right at the time. I think this could have really traumatized my brother. He trusted me, I told a terrible, harmful lie, and this could have stayed with him forever. I think at the time I thought that my great-grandmother was scary enough that, even though she didn't kill my great-grandfather, she was someone who believably could have. I, of course, know now that isn't true at all. She was just an older person who had weird views and was very much a product of her time. I also loved torturing my little brother, especially psychologically, at the time. I don't know why I did. I love my brother now! I did tell my little brother later on that this was not true, and, naturally, he was upset again. </p><p><br></p><p>Rather recently, I shared my remorse with my little brother in adulthood. He said that it wasn't that big of a deal and he forgave me. I disagree that it wasn't a big deal, but I am so glad that he considers it not a big deal. I do wonder what harm it caused. I am hoping that he was so young that maybe it was just one of those things that he couldn't even fully comprehend so it didn't impact as much as if he really understood concepts like death and murder. </p><p><br></p><p>For me, the kid that did this feels so far away from the adult I am now. I also was only mean to my little brother for the most part as a child. I was nice to everyone else, and very well-behaved otherwise! More than that, I think I was overall pretty kind and focused on trying to do what is right. This was, obviously, though, very wrong. I certainly am not at risk of doing anything like this again, but it's weird to know that I did do this. I think this makes me realize that people really can change as moral agents. While I think there are clearly some things that are morally wrong and morally right, people themselves are more complicated. </p>"}]}
